```ts
                new FlexContainer({ layout: { direction: 'column', gap: 10, alignItems: 'center' } }),
            );
            const box = column.add(new FlexContainer());
            const frame = new SilkGraphics();
            const clip = new SilkGraphics();

            // the box outline, drawn at its size
            box.add(frame, {
                position: 'absolute',
                inset: 0,
                onSize: (w, h) => {
                    frame.clear().roundRect(0, 0, w, h, 10).fill(INK.surface).stroke({ width: 1, color: INK.line });
                    clip.clear().roundRect(0, 0, w, h, 10).fill(0xffffff);
                },
            });
            // the mask clips cover and fill to the box. It goes on the box, not on the child:
            // a masked child measures only what its mask shows.
            box.add(clip, { position: 'absolute', inset: 0, onSize: () => {} });
            box.mask = clip;
            column.add(label(title));

            return box;
        };

        for (const fit of MODES) {
            const box = makeBox(fit);

            boxes.push({ box, art: box.add(artwork(), { fit }) });
        }
        // text shrink-to-fit: scale-down with a minimum scale
        const textBox = makeBox('scale-down text');
        const text = textBox.add(
            new SilkText({ text: 'Quarterly report', fontWeight: 800, fontSize: 30, fill: INK.text }),
            {
                fit: 'scale-down',
                minScale: 0.35,
            },
        );

        stage.addChild(grid);
        grid.resize(720, 360);

        let last = '';

        tick(() => {
            const key = JSON.stringify(params);

            if (key === last) return;
            last = key;
            const fitAlign = ALIGN[params.align];

            for (const { box, art } of boxes) {
                box.setStyle({ width: params.width, height: params.height });
                box.setLayout(art, { ...box.getLayout(art), fitAlign });
            }
            textBox.setStyle({ width: params.width, height: params.height });
            textBox.setLayout(text, { ...textBox.getLayout(text), fitAlign });
        });

        return () => grid.destroy({ children: true });
    },
});
```

`fit` scales a child into its box, like CSS `object-fit`.

| Value | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `none` | Keeps the child's size. The default |
| `contain` | Fits inside the box |
| `cover` | Fills the box and overflows. Mask the box, not the child: a masked child measures only what its mask shows |
| `scale-down` | `contain` that never enlarges |
| `fill` | Stretches both axes |

`fitAlign` places the child in its box, from `[0, 0]` (top left) to `[1, 1]` (bottom right). The
default is the centre. `minScale` and `maxScale` clamp the fit scale.

Text shrink-to-fit is `fit: 'scale-down'` with a floor. The text keeps its size while it fits and
scales down when its box gets narrow, never below `minScale`:

```ts

const pill = new FlexContainer({ layout: { direction: 'column', padding: [10, 18] } });

pill.add(new SilkText({ text: '$1,250,000.00', fontSize: 30 }), { fit: 'scale-down', minScale: 0.55 });
```

### Responsive styles

Any style can carry `portrait` and `landscape` values, and `media` rules. A rule matches an
`orientation`, a `breakpoint` name, or a `minWidth` and `maxWidth` in design units. Rules apply in
order over the base values. Width and orientation rules test the root container's layout area.

```ts

const main = new FlexContainer({
    layout: {
        gap: 20,
        landscape: { direction: 'row' },
        portrait: { direction: 'column' },
        media: [{ maxWidth: 600, style: { gap: 10 } }],
    },
});
const banner = new FlexContainer();

main.add(banner, { landscape: { display: 'none' } });
```

The banner shows in portrait only. A child with `display: 'none'` is hidden as well as taken out of the flow.

### Debug and preview devices

```ts

const debug = layoutDebug(app, { key: 'KeyL' });
const sim = simulateViewport({ area: app.screen, device: 'phone portrait' });
const hud = new SmartContainer({ viewport: sim, portraitData: { width: 1080, height: 300, valign: 'bottom' } });

sim.content.addChild(hud);
app.stage.addChild(sim);
sim.setDevice('phone landscape');
```

`layoutDebug` draws over the stage and is off until you toggle it with the key. For a
SmartContainer it shows the design box with its breakpoint and scale, the reference resolution in
purple, the safe area in orange and stretched axes in yellow. For a FlexContainer it shows boxes in
blue, padding in green, gaps in pink and real bounds in white, or red when a child overflows.

`simulateViewport` shows a device window inside a desktop page, scaled to fit and letterboxed.
Pass it as the `viewport` of your SmartContainers and add them to its `content`. `DEVICES` lists
the preset sizes, and a `[width, height]` pair works too.

### Where it stops

The layout runs on the CPU in your frame, once per change. Rotated children keep their box but
are measured unrotated. The engine has no `order`, `overflow` or borders, and an item's default
minimum size is 0, not its content. [Layout engine](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/layout-engine/#differences-from-css)
lists every difference from CSS.

---

# Canvas and rulers

URL: https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/canvas-and-rulers/
Area: Editor kit

> Pan and zoom a PixiJS scene with a camera, then add a grid, rulers, draggable guides, redlines, a marquee and equal-gap markers around it.

> **NOTE**
> Experimental in 1.0: the API may change in a minor release.

Demo sources run in the docs demo runner, `defineDemo({ setup({ stage, params, tick }) })`. In your own app, `stage` is `app.stage` (or a container on it), `params` holds the values of the demo controls, and `tick(fn)` is `app.ticker.add`, with `t` and `dt` in seconds.

Live demo `canvas-tools` (source):

```ts
import { Container, type FederatedPointerEvent } from 'pixi.js';
import { linear, SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { silkLabel } from 'pixi-silk/text';
import { Camera, ZoomControl } from 'pixi-silk/ui/camera';
import { TransformGizmo } from 'pixi-silk/ui/gizmo';
import { CanvasGrid } from 'pixi-silk/ui/grid';
import { SelectionGroup } from 'pixi-silk/ui/group';
import { Guides } from 'pixi-silk/ui/guides';
import { altDown } from 'pixi-silk/ui/keys';
import { Marquee } from 'pixi-silk/ui/marquee';
import { Measure } from 'pixi-silk/ui/measure';
import { boundsQuad, SelectionOutline, unionQuad } from 'pixi-silk/ui/outline';
import { Ruler } from 'pixi-silk/ui/ruler';
import { SnapEngine, type SnapRect } from 'pixi-silk/ui/snap';
import { findSpacing, SpacingMarkers } from 'pixi-silk/ui/spacing';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Drag on empty canvas to select, Shift adds and Alt takes away. Drag selected layers to move
// them: pink markers show equal gaps. Drag guides out of the rulers, drop them back to remove.
// Scroll to pan, pinch or ctrl + scroll to zoom.
export default defineDemo({
    title: 'Canvas tools',
    size: [760, 520],
    touchAction: 'none',
    ownsCamera: true,
    controls: {
        grid: { type: 'select', label: 'grid', options: ['lines', 'dots', 'off'], value: 'lines' },
        marquee: { type: 'select', label: 'marquee', options: ['intersect', 'contain'], value: 'intersect' },
    },
    setup({ silk, params }) {
        const app = silk.app;
        const world = new Container();
        const overlay = new Container();
        const camera = new Camera({ world, element: app.canvas });
        const grid = new CanvasGrid({ camera, ticker: app.ticker });
        // SilkText labels: distance fields stay sharp at any zoom
        const label = silkLabel();
        const ruler = new Ruler({ camera, ticker: app.ticker, label });
        const engine = new SnapEngine();
        const guides = new Guides({ camera, ruler, snap: engine, ticker: app.ticker, label });
        const group = new SelectionGroup();
        const hover = new SelectionOutline({ ticker: app.ticker });
        const outline = new SelectionOutline({ ticker: app.ticker, brackets: false });
        const spacing = new SpacingMarkers({ space: world, ticker: app.ticker });
        const measure = new Measure({ space: world, ticker: app.ticker, label });
        const layers: Container[] = [];
        let selected: readonly Container[] = [];
        let hovered: Container | null = null;

        const rectOf = (items: readonly Container[]): SnapRect => {
            const q = unionQuad(items.map((t) => boundsQuad(t, world)));

            return { x: q[0].x, y: q[0].y, width: q[2].x - q[0].x, height: q[2].y - q[0].y };
        };
        const others = () => layers.filter((l) => !selected.includes(l)).map((l) => rectOf([l]));
        const hooks = group.hooks();
        const gizmo = new TransformGizmo({
            mode: 'box',
            snapEngine: engine,
            ticker: app.ticker,
            onStart: () => {
                hooks.onStart();
                // snap to everything that is not moving, and to the guides
                engine.targets = others();
            },
            onChange: (_t, handle) => {
                hooks.onChange();
                if (handle === 'box-move') spacing.show(findSpacing(rectOf(selected), engine.targets));
            },
            onEnd: () => {
                hooks.onEnd();
                spacing.clear();
            },
        });
        const marquee = new Marquee({
            camera,
            surface: app.stage,
            candidates: () => layers,
            selection: () => selected,
            ticker: app.ticker,
            // live while dragging: only the outline follows, the gizmo comes when it ends
            onChange: (list) => (marquee.state.active.value ? preview(list) : select(list)),
            onEnd: (list) => select(list),
        });
        const zoom = new ZoomControl(camera, { label });

        world.addChild(group);
        guides.bindSnap(engine);
        camera.state.zoom.subscribe((k) => {
            engine.threshold = 6 / k;
        });
        engine.threshold = 6 / camera.zoom;
        overlay.addChild(hover, outline, spacing, measure, gizmo, guides, marquee, ruler, zoom);
        app.stage.addChild(grid, world, overlay);
        app.stage.eventMode = 'static';
        app.stage.hitArea = app.screen;

        function preview(list: readonly Container[]) {
            selected = list;
            outline.select(list);
            ruler.highlight(list.length ? list : null);
        }
        function select(list: readonly Container[]) {
            preview(list);
            // the gizmo's box frames the selection from here on
            outline.select([]);
            group.set(list);
            gizmo.attach(group.target);
            measure.size(list.length ? group.target : null);
            sync();
        }
        const sync = () => {
            const pair = altDown() && selected.length === 1 && hovered && hovered !== selected[0];

            measure.between(pair ? selected[0] : null, pair ? hovered : null);
            hover.hover(pair || (hovered && selected.includes(hovered)) ? null : hovered);
        };

        // an artboard: a frame you can marquee across, with layers on it
        const board = new SilkGraphics().rect(0, 0, 480, 360).fill(0xf4f4f6);

        world.addChildAt(board, 0);
        const layer = (x: number, y: number, draw: (g: SilkGraphics) => void) => {
            const item = new Container();
            const g = new SilkGraphics();

            draw(g);
            item.addChild(g);
            item.position.set(x, y);
            item.eventMode = 'static';
            item.cursor = 'pointer';
            item.on('pointerover', () => {
                hovered = item;
                sync();
            });
            item.on('pointerout', () => {
                if (hovered === item) hovered = null;
                sync();
            });
            item.on('pointerdown', (e: FederatedPointerEvent) => {
                if (e.button !== 0) return;
                e.stopPropagation();
                if (e.shiftKey)
                    select(selected.includes(item) ? selected.filter((s) => s !== item) : [...selected, item]);
                else if (!selected.includes(item)) select([item]);
                // press and move in one go
                if (selected.includes(item)) gizmo.grab(e);
            });
            world.addChildAt(item, world.children.length - 1);
            layers.push(item);

            return item;
        };

        layer(32, 32, (g) =>
            g.roundRect(0, 0, 416, 120, 24, 0.6).fill(linear([0x5e5ce6, 0xbf5af2], { from: [0, 0], to: [1, 1] })),
        );
        const a = layer(32, 184, (g) => g.roundRect(0, 0, 120, 96, 18, 0.6).fill(0xffffff));

        layer(180, 184, (g) => g.roundRect(0, 0, 120, 96, 18, 0.6).fill(0xffffff));
        layer(360, 184, (g) => g.roundRect(0, 0, 88, 96, 18, 0.6).fill(0xffffff));
        layer(32, 304, (g) => g.circle(12, 12, 12).fill(0x30d158));
        layer(72, 308, (g) => g.pill(0, 0, 160, 16).fill(0x1c1c1e));

        guides.add('x', 32);
        guides.add('y', 184);

        const place = () =>
            zoom.position.set(app.screen.width - zoom.size[0] - 12, app.screen.height - zoom.size[1] - 12);

        app.renderer.on('resize', place);
        place();
        select([a]);
        camera.fit({ x: -40, y: -40, width: 560, height: 440 }, 24, false);

        const onKey = (e: KeyboardEvent) => e.key === 'Alt' && sync();

        addEventListener('keydown', onKey);
        addEventListener('keyup', onKey);
        app.ticker.add(() => {
            grid.state.visible.set(params.grid !== 'off');
            if (params.grid !== 'off') grid.state.style.set(params.grid as 'lines' | 'dots');
            marquee.mode = params.marquee as 'intersect' | 'contain';
        });

        return () => {
            removeEventListener('keydown', onKey);
            removeEventListener('keyup', onKey);
            camera.destroy();
            ruler.destroy();
            marquee.destroy();
        };
    },
});
```

The camera pans and zooms a world container. The other pieces on this page read the camera and
draw in screen space around it. Each one is its own `pixi-silk/ui/<name>` entry point, and none of
them holds a selection. They take plain arrays of containers and report back through callbacks,
so any selection store can drive them. [Gizmos and selection](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/gizmos-and-selection/) adds
one.

### Pan and zoom with a camera

```ts

const world = new Container();
const overlay = new Container();
const camera = new Camera({ world, element: app.canvas });

app.stage.addChild(world, overlay);
overlay.addChild(new ZoomControl(camera));
camera.fit();
```

The camera moves and scales `world`. Put overlays beside the world, not inside it: outlines,
gizmos and redlines on the overlay follow the objects and keep their size on screen.

A trackpad pinch, or ctrl or cmd with the wheel, zooms around the cursor. Scrolling pans, and so
do middle drag, right drag and Space + drag. On touch screens two fingers pinch and pan, so one
finger stays with the scene. Pans never reach the scene, so your own clicks and drags keep working.

| Option | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `zoomRange` | `[0.02, 256]` | Smallest and largest zoom. |
| `wheel` | `'pan'` | `'zoom'` makes the wheel zoom and Shift + wheel pan, like a map. |
| `panButtons` | `[1, 2]` | Mouse buttons that pan when dragged: 1 is middle, 2 is right. |
| `spacePan` | `true` | Holding Space turns a left drag into a pan. |
| `touchPan` | `'two'` | `'one'` pans with one finger. |
| `inertia` | `true` | A fling keeps gliding and slows down. |
| `keys` | `true` | `+` and `-` zoom, Shift + 0 is 100% and Shift + 1 fits the content. |
| `pageScroll` | `'auto'` | In a page that scrolls, a plain scroll keeps scrolling the page until the canvas is clicked. `false` gives the canvas every scroll. |
| `ignore` | none | Points the camera leaves alone, such as panels drawn over the view. |

`fit()`, `zoomTo()`, `zoomBy()` and `centerOn()` animate unless you pass `false`. `fit()` takes
padding per side, like `{ left: 280, right: 300 }`, to centre content between docked panels.
`state.zoom`, `state.x` and `state.y` are signals for readouts and for anything that redraws on
zoom. `screenToWorld()` and `worldToScreen()` convert points.

### Measure distances

`Measure` draws redlines from one object to another, like holding Alt in Figma. Side by side it
shows the gap along the band the two share. When one sits inside the other it shows the margin on
every side. `size()` adds a `W x H` badge.

```ts

declare const world: Container, overlay: Container, selected: Container, hovered: Container;

const measure = new Measure({ space: world });

overlay.addChild(measure);
measure.size(selected);
measure.between(selected, hovered);
```

Distances are in `space`, usually the camera's world, so a gap of 24 reads 24 at any zoom.
Redlines follow their objects every frame. `format` turns a value into a label, for units or
rounding.

### Draw a grid

```ts

const world = new Container();
const camera = new Camera({ world, element: app.canvas });
const grid = new CanvasGrid({ camera, style: 'dots', axes: true });

app.stage.addChild(grid, world);
```

`CanvasGrid` draws an infinite grid behind the world. Levels nest at 1, 10 and 100 world units,
and each one fades in as its lines spread apart on screen, so zooming never pops a level in.
A level of lines costs two shapes, whatever the size of the view. Dots take one shape per row.

| Option | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `style` | `'lines'` | `'lines'` or `'dots'`. Change it later through `state.style` and the grid crossfades. |
| `unit` | `1` | Finest step, in world units. |
| `ratio` | `10` | How many times each level is the one below. |
| `fade` | `[6, 120]` | Screen spacing where a level starts to show and where it reaches full strength. |
| `axes` | `false` | Draws the x = 0 and y = 0 axes. |

`gridLevels()` returns the levels for a zoom, for your own drawing.

### Add rulers and guides

```ts

declare const camera: Camera, overlay: Container, selected: Container[];

const ruler = new Ruler({ camera });
const engine = new SnapEngine();
const guides = new Guides({ camera, ruler, snap: engine });

overlay.addChild(guides, ruler);
ruler.highlight(selected);
guides.bindSnap(engine);
```

`Ruler` draws a top and a left ruler. Labels step in 1, 2 and 5 times a power of ten. When the
step changes, the labels the two steps share stay put while the rest fade. `highlight()` bands the
extent of objects or a world rectangle in the accent colour. `format` turns world values into
labels, for units such as `pt` or `mm`.

`Guides` lets the reader drag a guide out of a ruler: the top ruler gives a horizontal guide, the
left one a vertical guide. Dropping a guide back on the ruler removes it. While dragging, a guide
snaps to the edges and centres of the engine's targets, and Ctrl or Cmd turns snapping off.
`bindSnap()` feeds the guides back into the engine, so objects snap to them too. `add()`, `move()`,
`remove()` and `clear()` edit guides from code, and `state.guides` is a signal of all of them.

### Select with a marquee

```ts

declare const camera: Camera, overlay: Container, layers: Container[];
let selected: readonly Container[] = [];

app.stage.eventMode = 'static';
app.stage.hitArea = app.screen;
const marquee = new Marquee({
    camera,
    surface: app.stage,
    candidates: () => layers,
    selection: () => selected,
    onChange: (next) => {
        selected = next;
    },
});

overlay.addChild(marquee);
```

A left drag that starts on `surface` itself draws the rectangle. Shift adds to the selection and
Alt takes away. The camera keeps middle, right, Space and two-finger drags, and a pan that starts
mid-drag cancels the marquee. Near the edge of the view the camera pans.

`mode: 'intersect'` (the default) picks what the rectangle touches, rotated objects included.
`mode: 'contain'` picks what is fully inside. For a spatial index or custom shapes, pass
`hit: (rect, mode) => targets` instead of `candidates`.

### Show equal spacing

Live demo `smart-spacing` (source):

```ts
import { Container, type FederatedPointerEvent, type PointData } from 'pixi.js';
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { SnapEngine, type SnapRect } from 'pixi-silk/ui/snap';
import { findSpacing, gapsBetween, SpacingMarkers } from 'pixi-silk/ui/spacing';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Drag a card: it snaps to equal gaps and shared edges, and pink markers show every equal gap.
// Hover the stack on the right to see its spacing, the way an auto-layout frame shows it.
export default defineDemo({
    title: 'Smart spacing',
    size: [760, 380],
    touchAction: 'none',
    controls: {
        snap: { type: 'toggle', label: 'snap', value: true },
    },
    setup({ stage, params }) {
        const scene = new Container();
        const markers = new SpacingMarkers({ space: scene });
        const stack = new Container();
        const engine = new SnapEngine({ threshold: 8 });
        const cards: Container[] = [];
        const rect = (c: Container): SnapRect => {
            const b = c.getLocalBounds();

            return { x: c.x + b.minX, y: c.y + b.minY, width: b.width, height: b.height };
        };
        let drag: { card: Container; grab: PointData; id: number } | null = null;

        stage.addChild(scene, markers);
        stage.eventMode = 'static';
        stage.hitArea = { contains: () => true };

        const card = (x: number, y: number, w: number, h: number, color: number) => {
            const c = new Container();

            c.addChild(new SilkGraphics().roundRect(0, 0, w, h, 14, 0.6).fill(color));
            c.position.set(x, y);
            c.eventMode = 'static';
            c.cursor = 'grab';
            c.on('pointerdown', (e: FederatedPointerEvent) => {
                const p = scene.toLocal(e.global);

                drag = { card: c, grab: { x: p.x - c.x, y: p.y - c.y }, id: e.pointerId };
                engine.targets = cards.filter((o) => o !== c).map(rect);
            });
            scene.addChild(c);
            cards.push(c);

            return c;
        };

        card(40, 60, 110, 80, 0x0a84ff);
        card(190, 60, 110, 80, 0x5e5ce6);
        card(380, 80, 90, 60, 0xbf5af2);
        card(110, 220, 140, 90, 0x30d158);

        stage.on('globalpointermove', (e: FederatedPointerEvent) => {
            if (!drag || e.pointerId !== drag.id) return;
            const p = scene.toLocal(e.global);
            const c = drag.card;
            const b = c.getLocalBounds();
            const moving = {
                x: p.x - drag.grab.x + b.minX,
                y: p.y - drag.grab.y + b.minY,
                width: b.width,
                height: b.height,
            };
            const snap = params.snap ? engine.snap(moving) : { dx: 0, dy: 0 };

            c.position.set(p.x - drag.grab.x + snap.dx, p.y - drag.grab.y + snap.dy);
            // what lines up after snapping
            markers.show(findSpacing(rect(c), engine.targets));
        });
        const end = () => {
            drag = null;
            markers.clear();
        };

        stage.on('pointerup', end);
        stage.on('pointerupoutside', end);

        // an auto-layout stack: its children's gaps on hover
        stack.position.set(560, 40);
        for (const [i, h] of [56, 40, 72].entries()) {
            const row = new SilkGraphics().roundRect(0, 0, 150, h, 10, 0.6).fill(0x2c2c2e);

            row.y = [0, 72, 128][i];
            stack.addChild(row);
        }
        stack.eventMode = 'static';
        stack.on('pointerover', () => {
            const rows = stack.children.map((r) => ({ x: stack.x, y: stack.y + r.y, width: 150, height: r.height }));

            markers.show({ gaps: gapsBetween(rows, 'y') });
        });
        stack.on('pointerout', () => markers.clear());
        scene.addChild(stack);

        return undefined;
    },
});
```

`findSpacing()` looks at a box among others. When the gap to a neighbour equals another gap in the
same row or column, it returns every one of those gaps. It also returns edges and centres that
line up. `SpacingMarkers` draws them as segments with value pills.

```ts

declare const world: Container, overlay: Container, moving: SnapRect, others: SnapRect[];

const engine = new SnapEngine({ targets: others });
const markers = new SpacingMarkers({ space: world });

overlay.addChild(markers);
const snap = engine.snap(moving);

markers.show(findSpacing({ ...moving, x: moving.x + snap.dx, y: moving.y + snap.dy }, others));
```

Snap first, then report on the snapped box, or the markers show gaps the box is about to leave.
`gapsBetween()` reads the gaps of a row or a column, so an auto-layout container can show its
spacing with the same markers.

### Labels

`Measure`, `Ruler`, `Guides` and `ZoomControl` draw their text with PixiJS `Text` by default.
Pass `label: silkLabel()` from `pixi-silk/text` to draw it with [SilkText](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/text/) instead.
The widgets never import SilkText on their own, so an app that skips the option does not ship it.

```ts

declare const camera: Camera, world: Container;

const label = silkLabel();
const measure = new Measure({ space: world, label });
const ruler = new Ruler({ camera, label });
const zoom = new ZoomControl(camera, { label });
```

### Where it stops

The pieces draw and report. They do not keep a document, a layer list or an undo stack.
[Gizmos and selection](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/gizmos-and-selection/) adds a selection store and a history, and
[Panels](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/panels/) adds a layer tree and an inspector.

---

# Gizmos and selection

URL: https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/gizmos-and-selection/
Area: Editor kit

> Move, rotate and resize PixiJS objects with a gizmo, snap them to smart guides, track the selection and undo every drag with a history stack.

> **NOTE**
> Experimental in 1.0: the API may change in a minor release.

Demo sources run in the docs demo runner, `defineDemo({ setup({ stage, params, tick }) })`. In your own app, `stage` is `app.stage` (or a container on it), `params` holds the values of the demo controls, and `tick(fn)` is `app.ticker.add`, with `t` and `dt` in seconds.

Live demo `gizmo` (source):

```ts
import { Container } from 'pixi.js';
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { type GizmoMode, type GizmoSpace, TransformGizmo } from 'pixi-silk/ui/gizmo';
import { SelectionOutline } from 'pixi-silk/ui/outline';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Click a shape to select it, then drag the handles. Hold Shift to snap.
export default defineDemo({
    title: 'Transform gizmo',
    size: [640, 420],
    touchAction: 'none',
    controls: {
        mode: {
            type: 'select',
            label: 'mode',
            options: ['universal', 'move', 'rotate', 'scale', 'box'],
            value: 'universal',
        },
        space: { type: 'select', label: 'space', options: ['local', 'world'], value: 'local' },
        snap: { type: 'toggle', label: 'always snap', value: false },
    },
    setup({ silk, stage, params, tick }) {
        const ticker = silk.app.ticker;
        const scene = new Container();
        const overlay = new Container();
        const background = new Container();
        const hover = new SelectionOutline({ ticker });
        const selection = new SelectionOutline({ ticker });
        const gizmo = new TransformGizmo({ ticker });

        background.eventMode = 'static';
        background.hitArea = { contains: () => true };
        background.on('pointerdown', () => {
            gizmo.attach(null);
            selection.select(null);
        });
        stage.addChild(background, scene, overlay);
        overlay.addChild(hover, selection, gizmo);

        const shape = (x: number, y: number, rotation: number, draw: (g: SilkGraphics) => void) => {
            const item = new Container();
            const g = new SilkGraphics();

            draw(g);
            item.addChild(g);
            item.position.set(x, y);
            item.rotation = rotation;
            item.eventMode = 'static';
            item.cursor = 'pointer';
            item.on('pointerover', () => hover.hover(item));
            item.on('pointerout', () => hover.hover(null));
            item.on('pointerdown', (e) => {
                e.stopPropagation();
                selection.select(item);
                gizmo.attach(item);
            });
            scene.addChild(item);

            return item;
        };

        const card = shape(190, 210, -0.2, (g) => g.roundRect(-70, -45, 140, 90, 18, 0.6).fill(0x30d158));

        shape(400, 150, 0.3, (g) => g.star(0, 0, 5, 52, undefined, 0, 6).fill(0xffd60a));
        shape(460, 300, 0, (g) => g.roundRect(-55, -30, 110, 60, 30).fill(0xbf5af2));
        selection.select(card);
        gizmo.attach(card);

        tick(() => {
            gizmo.state.mode.set(params.mode as GizmoMode);
            gizmo.state.space.set(params.space as GizmoSpace);
            // the box mode draws its own frame
            selection.alpha = params.mode === 'box' ? 0 : 1;
            gizmo.snap.enabled = params.snap;
        });

        return undefined;
    },
});
```

A `TransformGizmo` moves, rotates and scales one container. A `SelectionOutline` frames what is
selected, and a `SnapEngine` says where a moving box should land. A `Selection` store and a
`History` tie them together. Each piece works alone and has its own entry point.

### Attach a gizmo

```ts

const overlay = new Container();
const shape = new Container();
const gizmo = new TransformGizmo({
    mode: 'universal',
    snap: { translate: 10, angle: Math.PI / 12, scale: 0.1 },
    onChange: (t) => console.log(t.x, t.y, t.rotation, t.scaleX, t.scaleY),
});

overlay.addChild(gizmo);
gizmo.attach(shape);
gizmo.state.mode.set('rotate');
```

Put the gizmo in an overlay above the scene. It reads the target's transform every frame, so it
follows the target wherever it sits in the tree. It writes the new transform back into the target
unless you pass `apply: false`.

| Mode | Handles |
|---|---|
| `move` | Arrows lock to one axis, the centre square moves freely. |
| `rotate` | A ring turns the object about its pivot, with the swept angle in a pill. |
| `scale` | Cube handles stretch one axis, the centre ring scales evenly. |
| `box` | Eight handles resize in the object's own frame. Shift keeps the aspect, Alt resizes from the centre. A drag right outside a corner rotates. |
| `universal` | Move, rotate and scale handles together. This is the default. |

| Option | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `space` | `'local'` | Moves along the object's own axes, or `'world'`. Scale handles always follow the object's axes. |
| `snap` | `{ translate: 10, angle: 15 degrees, scale: 0.1 }` | Steps Shift snaps to. `snap.enabled: true` snaps by default, and Shift then turns it off. |
| `snapEngine`, `snapGuides` | none | Smart guides for free moves, below. |
| `readout` | `true` | Shows the angle, size or offset in a pill while dragging. |
| `transition` | `'pop'` | When the gizmo moves to another object, the old handles shrink away and the new ones spring in. `'glide'` slides them over. |
| `apply` | `true` | `false` leaves the target alone, so you apply the change in `onChange`, for undo stacks or networked editors. |

`gizmo.grab(event)` starts a move from a press on the object itself, so one gesture selects and
drags an unselected shape. The box handles assume the object is not mirrored.

### Outline the selection

```ts

const [shape, a, b] = [new Container(), new Container(), new Container()];
const hover = new SelectionOutline();
const selected = new SelectionOutline({ dash: [5, 4], march: 16, padding: 4 });

shape.on('pointerover', () => hover.hover(shape));
shape.on('pointerout', () => hover.hover(null));
selected.select([a, b]);
```

`hover()` draws a quiet line. `select()` draws marching dashes and corner brackets. A rotated
object gets a rotated box, and several objects get one box around all of them. `march: 0` keeps
the dashes still.

### Snap to smart guides

Live demo `snap` (source):

```ts
import { Container } from 'pixi.js';
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { TransformGizmo } from 'pixi-silk/ui/gizmo';
import { boundsQuad, SelectionOutline } from 'pixi-silk/ui/outline';
import { SnapEngine, SnapGuides } from 'pixi-silk/ui/snap';

import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Drag a card: edges and centres line up with the others, and equal gaps show their size.
// Hold Ctrl or Cmd to move without guides.
export default defineDemo({
    title: 'Snapping',
    size: [640, 380],
    touchAction: 'none',
    controls: {
        spacing: { type: 'toggle', label: 'equal spacing', value: true },
        threshold: { type: 'range', label: 'threshold', min: 2, max: 16, step: 1, value: 6 },
    },
    setup({ silk, stage, params, tick }) {
        const ticker = silk.app.ticker;
        const scene = new Container();
        const engine = new SnapEngine({ lines: { x: [320], y: [190] } });
        const guides = new SnapGuides({ ticker });
        const selection = new SelectionOutline({ ticker });
        const gizmo = new TransformGizmo({ ticker, mode: 'box', snapEngine: engine, snapGuides: guides });
        const cards: Container[] = [];
        const colors = [0x0a84ff, 0xff9f0a, 0x30d158, 0xbf5af2];
        const select = (card: Container | null) => {
            selection.select(card);
            gizmo.attach(card);
        };

        stage.eventMode = 'static';
        stage.hitArea = { contains: () => true };
        stage.on('pointerdown', () => select(null));
        stage.addChild(scene, guides, selection, gizmo);
        [
            [70, 80],
            [250, 80],
            [430, 120],
            [140, 240],
        ].forEach(([x, y], i) => {
            const card = new Container();
            const g = new SilkGraphics();

            g.roundRect(0, 0, i === 2 ? 140 : 110, 80, 14, 0.6).fill(colors[i]);
            card.addChild(g);
            card.position.set(x, y);
            card.eventMode = 'static';
            card.cursor = 'move';
            card.on('pointerdown', (e) => {
                e.stopPropagation();
                select(card);
                // press and drag in one go, even on a card that was not selected yet
                gizmo.grab(e);
            });
            scene.addChild(card);
            cards.push(card);
        });
        select(cards[3]);

        // what to snap against: every card, read as rectangles in the scene's space
        const refresh = () => {
            engine.targets = cards.map((c) => {
                const q = boundsQuad(c, scene);
                const xs = q.map((p) => p.x);
                const ys = q.map((p) => p.y);

                return {
                    x: Math.min(...xs),
                    y: Math.min(...ys),
                    width: Math.max(...xs) - Math.min(...xs),
                    height: Math.max(...ys) - Math.min(...ys),
                };
            });
        };

        gizmo.state.dragging.subscribe((on) => on && refresh());
        refresh();
        tick(() => {
            engine.spacing = params.spacing;
            engine.threshold = params.threshold;
        });

        return undefined;
    },
});
```

```ts

const others = [{ x: 0, y: 0, width: 100, height: 60 }];
const engine = new SnapEngine({ threshold: 6, targets: others, lines: { x: [320] } });
const box = { x: 103, y: 40, width: 80, height: 60 };
const r = engine.snap(box);

box.x += r.dx;
box.y += r.dy;
```

`SnapEngine` is maths with no drawing. It aligns edges with edges and centres with centres, and it
offers equal spacing: centred between two neighbours, or at a gap that already exists in the row.
`SnapGuides` draws the result as dashed lines with gap sizes in pills. Pass both to a gizmo as
`snapEngine` and `snapGuides`, and its free moves snap on their own.

### Move several objects at once

```ts

declare const world: Container, overlay: Container, selected: Container[];

const group = new SelectionGroup();
const gizmo = new TransformGizmo({ mode: 'box', ...group.hooks() });

world.addChild(group);
overlay.addChild(gizmo);
group.set(selected);
gizmo.attach(group.target);
```

`SelectionGroup` is an invisible box around its objects. Each change of the box applies to every
object about the common pivot, whatever their parents. With one object selected, `group.target`
is that object, so it keeps its own turned frame. Call `refit()` after the objects move on their
own.

### Track the selection

Live demo `selection-history` (source):

```ts
import { Container, Text } from 'pixi.js';
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { bindHistoryKeys, History, recordGizmo, recordSignals } from 'pixi-silk/history';
import { bindGizmo, bindOutline, Selection } from 'pixi-silk/selection';
import { type Signal, signal, watch } from 'pixi-silk/signal';
import { SilkButton } from 'pixi-silk/ui/button';
import { TransformGizmo } from 'pixi-silk/ui/gizmo';
import { SelectionOutline } from 'pixi-silk/ui/outline';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

const COLORS = [0x0a84ff, 0x30d158, 0xff9f0a, 0xff375f, 0xbf5af2, 0x64d2ff];

// Click a shape, Shift + click to add more, then drag the gizmo. Circles selects by a test.
// Tint recolours the selection through signals, recorded without writing a command.
// Every step lands in the list on the right. Cmd or ctrl + Z undoes, Shift + cmd + Z redoes.
export default defineDemo({
    title: 'Selection and history',
    size: [760, 420],
    touchAction: 'none',
    setup({ stage, silk }) {
        const ticker = silk.app.ticker;
        const scene = new Container();
        const overlay = new Container();
        const history = new History();
        const selection = new Selection<Container>();
        const outline = new SelectionOutline({ ticker });
        const hover = new SelectionOutline({ ticker });
        const gizmo = new TransformGizmo({ ticker, readout: false });
        const colors = new Map<Container, Signal<number>>();

        overlay.addChild(hover, outline, gizmo);
        stage.addChild(scene, overlay);
        stage.eventMode = 'static';
        stage.hitArea = { contains: (x: number, y: number) => x >= 0 && y >= 0 && x <= 760 && y <= 420 };
        // a press on the background (not on a button or a shape) clears the selection
        stage.on('pointerdown', (e) => e.target === stage && !e.shiftKey && selection.clear());

        const shape = (kind: 'circle' | 'square', x: number, y: number, i: number) => {
            const item = new Container({ label: kind });
            const g = new SilkGraphics();
            const color = signal(COLORS[i % COLORS.length]);

            watch(color, (c) => {
                g.clear();
                if (kind === 'circle') g.circle(0, 0, 38).fill(c);
                else g.roundRect(-36, -36, 72, 72, 18, 0.6).fill(c);
            });
            item.addChild(g);
            item.position.set(x, y);
            item.eventMode = 'static';
            item.cursor = 'pointer';
            item.on('pointerover', () => selection.state.hovered.set(item));
            item.on('pointerout', () => selection.state.hovered.set(null));
            item.on('pointerdown', (e) => {
                e.stopPropagation();
                selection.pick(item, e.shiftKey ? 'toggle' : 'replace');
                // press and drag in one go
                if (!e.shiftKey) gizmo.grab(e);
            });
            scene.addChild(item);
            colors.set(item, color);

            return item;
        };
        const items = [
            shape('circle', 110, 150, 0),
            shape('square', 240, 150, 1),
            shape('circle', 370, 150, 2),
            shape('square', 110, 290, 3),
            shape('circle', 240, 290, 4),
            shape('square', 370, 290, 5),
        ];
        const stops = [
            bindOutline(selection, outline, hover),
            bindGizmo(selection, gizmo),
            // one undo step per drag, and colour changes recorded from the signals
            recordGizmo(gizmo, history),
            recordSignals(history, [...colors.values()], { label: 'Tint' }).stop,
            bindHistoryKeys(history),
        ];

        // buttons
        const dark = { fill: 0x2c2c2e, fillHover: 0x3a3a3c, fillPress: 0x1c1c1e, fillDisabled: 0x232325 };
        let tint = 0;
        const button = (label: string, x: number, onPress: () => void) => {
            const b = new SilkButton({ ticker, label, width: 84, height: 32, theme: dark, onPress });

            b.position.set(x, 40);
            overlay.addChild(b);

            return b;
        };

        button('Circles', 66, () => selection.selectWhere(items, (s) => s.label === 'circle'));
        button('Tint', 160, () => {
            tint++;
            // one handler, one step: every colour set here undoes together
            for (const item of selection.items)
                colors.get(item)?.set(COLORS[(COLORS.indexOf(colors.get(item)?.value ?? 0) + tint) % COLORS.length]);
        });
        const undo = button('Undo', 254, () => history.undo());
        const redo = button('Redo', 348, () => history.redo());

        stops.push(
            watch(history.state.canUndo, (v) => undo.state.enabled.set(v)),
            watch(history.state.canRedo, (v) => redo.state.enabled.set(v)),
        );

        // the stack, newest at the bottom, undone steps greyed out
        const panel = new SilkGraphics();
        const list = new Container();

        panel.roundRect(470, 24, 266, 372, 18, 0.6).fill(0x1c1c1e);
        stage.addChildAt(panel, 0);
        stage.addChild(list);
        const style = (fill: number) => ({ fontFamily: 'Inter, system-ui, sans-serif', fontSize: 13, fill });
        const render = () => {
            list.removeChildren().forEach((c) => {
                c.destroy();
            });
            const rows = [
                ...history.past.slice(-8).map((s) => ({ label: s.label, done: true })),
                ...history.future.slice(0, 4).map((s) => ({ label: s.label, done: false })),
            ];
            const title = new Text({ text: 'History', style: style(0x8e8e93), resolution: 4 });

            title.position.set(494, 42);
            list.addChild(title);
            if (!rows.length) rows.push({ label: 'Nothing yet', done: false });
            rows.forEach((row, i) => {
                const t = new Text({ text: row.label, style: style(row.done ? 0xf2f2f7 : 0x636366), resolution: 4 });

                t.position.set(494, 74 + i * 26);
                list.addChild(t);
            });
        };

        stops.push(history.onChange(render));
        render();
        selection.pick(items[1]);

        return () => {
            for (const stop of stops) stop();
        };
    },
});
```

```ts

declare const layers: Container[];
declare const outline: SelectionOutline, hover: SelectionOutline;
declare const gizmo: TransformGizmo, measure: Measure;

const selection = new Selection<Container>();

for (const layer of layers) {
    layer.on('pointerdown', (e) => selection.pick(layer, e.shiftKey ? 'toggle' : 'replace'));
    layer.on('pointerover', () => selection.state.hovered.set(layer));
}
bindOutline(selection, outline, hover);
bindGizmo(selection, gizmo);
bindMeasure(selection, measure);
```

`Selection` keeps the items in the order they were picked, the primary item (the last one picked)
and the item under the pointer. All three are signals. `pick()` does what a click does,
`selectWhere(pool, test)` selects by a test and `focus(item)` changes the primary item without
changing the set. `filter` keeps locked layers out, and `multiple: false` allows one item at most.
The store takes any item type: containers, ids or your own records.

The `bind*` adapters make the widgets follow the store, and each returns the function that stops
it. `bindGizmo` attaches the gizmo to the primary item. `bindMeasure` shows its size and the
distances to the hovered item. The selection module draws nothing itself.

### Undo and redo

```ts

declare const gizmo: TransformGizmo, layer: { name: string };

const history = new History();

recordGizmo(gizmo, history);
bindHistoryKeys(history);
history.execute({ label: 'Rename', do: () => (layer.name = 'Hero'), undo: () => (layer.name = 'Layer 1') });
```

`History` is a stack of commands, each with `do()` and `undo()`. `recordGizmo` turns each gizmo
drag into one step labelled Move, Rotate, Scale or Resize. `bindHistoryKeys` binds the usual
shortcuts on `window` and leaves keys typed into inputs alone.

| Call | What it does |
|---|---|
| `execute(command)` | Runs a command and stores it. |
| `record(command)` | Stores a change that already happened, such as the end of a drag. |
| `begin()`, `end()` | Everything recorded in between becomes one step. They nest. |
| `transaction(label, fn)` | `begin()` and `end()` around `fn`, and reverts the changes if `fn` throws. |
| `merge(next)` on a command | Folds the next command into this one, so typing or nudges become one step. |

A new change drops the steps you could redo. `maxDepth` caps the stack at 200 steps by default.
`state.canUndo`, `state.canRedo`, `state.undoLabel` and `state.redoLabel` are signals for buttons
and menus.

#### Record signals instead of commands

```ts

const history = new History();
const doc = { title: signal('Untitled'), zoom: signal(1) };

recordSignals(history, doc);
doc.title.value = 'Poster';
history.undo();
```

You keep writing to the signals and undo works. Changes made in one handler become one step. The
default `patch` mode stores only the signals that changed, and `snapshot` stores all of them, for
values that depend on each other.

#### Use your own store

Widgets take any `HistoryLike`: an object with `record`, `begin` and `end`. An adapter over your
own state library sends the same edits there.

```ts

declare const store: { push(entry: { label?: string; undo(): void; redo(): void }): void };

const history: HistoryLike = {
    record: (c) => store.push({ label: c.label, undo: () => c.undo(), redo: () => c.do() }),
    begin: () => {},
    end: () => {},
};
```

---

# Path editing

URL: https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/path-editing/
Area: Editor kit

> Edit bezier paths in PixiJS: drag anchors and handles, add and delete points, draw new paths with a pen tool, and undo every edit as one step.

> **NOTE**
> Experimental in 1.0: the API may change in a minor release.

Demo sources run in the docs demo runner, `defineDemo({ setup({ stage, params, tick }) })`. In your own app, `stage` is `app.stage` (or a container on it), `params` holds the values of the demo controls, and `tick(fn)` is `app.ticker.add`, with `t` and `dt` in seconds.

Live demo `path-editing` (source):

```ts
import { Container, Text } from 'pixi.js';
import { linear, SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { bindHistoryKeys, History } from 'pixi-silk/history';
import { type BezierPath, drawPath, parseSvgPath, pathBounds } from 'pixi-silk/path';
import { watch } from 'pixi-silk/signal';
import { SilkButton } from 'pixi-silk/ui/button';
import { Camera } from 'pixi-silk/ui/camera';
import { SegmentedSwitch } from 'pixi-silk/ui/modes';
import { PathGizmo } from 'pixi-silk/ui/pathgizmo';
import { defineDemo, FONT, INK } from './runtime';

// Drag anchors and handles. Click the outline to add a point, double-click a point to switch it
// between corner and smooth, Alt + drag a handle to break the curve. The pen adds points: click
// for corners, drag for curves, click the first point to close. Cmd or ctrl + Z undoes.
export default defineDemo({
    title: 'Path editing',
    size: [720, 480],
    touchAction: 'none',
    ownsCamera: true,
    controls: {
        snap: { type: 'toggle', label: 'snap to 10', value: false },
    },
    setup({ silk, params }) {
        const app = silk.app;
        const world = new Container();
        const overlay = new Container();
        const camera = new Camera({ world, element: app.canvas });
        const history = new History();
        const shape = new SilkGraphics();
        const [heart] = parseSvgPath(
            'M0 -60 C 0 -140 -140 -140 -140 -40 C -140 60 0 120 0 180 C 0 120 140 60 140 -40 C 140 -140 0 -140 0 -60 Z',
        );
        const gizmo = new PathGizmo({ path: heart, space: world, history, ticker: app.ticker });
        const paint = linear([0xff375f, 0xbf5af2], { from: [0, 0], to: [1, 1] });

        world.addChild(shape);
        overlay.addChild(gizmo);
        app.stage.addChild(world, overlay);
        gizmo.snap = (p) => (params.snap ? { x: Math.round(p.x / 10) * 10, y: Math.round(p.y / 10) * 10 } : p);

        // the shape is drawn from the path: one exact fill, then the stroke
        const draw = (path: BezierPath) => {
            shape.clear();
            if (path.anchors.length < 2) return;
            if (path.closed) drawPath(shape, path).fill(paint);
            drawPath(shape, path).stroke({ width: 2, color: 0xf2f2f7, alpha: path.closed ? 0.35 : 0.9 });
        };
        const stops = [watch(gizmo.state.path, draw)];

        // a toolbar: tools, undo, redo and a fresh path for the pen
        const dark = { fill: 0x2c2c2e, fillHover: 0x3a3a3c, fillPress: 0x1c1c1e, fillDisabled: 0x232325 };
        const tools = new SegmentedSwitch({
            ticker: app.ticker,
            value: 'edit',
            segmentWidth: 52,
            height: 32,
            options: [
                { id: 'edit', label: 'Edit' },
                { id: 'pen', label: 'Pen' },
            ],
        });
        const button = (label: string, onPress: () => void) =>
            new SilkButton({ ticker: app.ticker, label, width: 60, height: 32, theme: dark, onPress });
        const undo = button('Undo', () => history.undo());
        const redo = button('Redo', () => history.redo());
        const fresh = button('New', () => {
            gizmo.edit('New path', () => ({ anchors: [], closed: false }), []);
            gizmo.state.tool.set('pen');
        });
        const hint = new Text({
            text: '',
            style: { fontFamily: FONT, fontSize: 12, fill: INK.muted },
            resolution: 4,
        });

        overlay.addChild(tools, undo, redo, fresh, hint);
        // one row from the left edge, 318 px wide in all: it fits a phone
        tools.position.set(68, 32);
        undo.position.set(156, 32);
        redo.position.set(222, 32);
        fresh.position.set(288, 32);
        const placeHint = () => hint.position.set(16, app.screen.height - 28);

        placeHint();
        app.renderer.on('resize', placeHint);
        stops.push(
            // the switch and the gizmo share one tool
            watch(tools.state.value, (v) => gizmo.state.tool.set(v === 'pen' ? 'pen' : 'edit')),
            watch(gizmo.state.tool, (v) => tools.state.value.set(v)),
            watch(history.state.canUndo, (v) => undo.state.enabled.set(v)),
            watch(history.state.canRedo, (v) => redo.state.enabled.set(v)),
            watch(history.state.undoLabel, (label) => {
                hint.text = label
                    ? `Undo ${label.toLowerCase()} with cmd or ctrl + Z`
                    : 'Edit the heart, or press New to draw';
            }),
            bindHistoryKeys(history),
        );

        const b = pathBounds(heart);

        camera.fit(b, { left: 48, right: 48, top: 72, bottom: 48 }, false);

        return () => {
            for (const stop of stops) stop();
            app.renderer.off('resize', placeHint);
            camera.destroy();
        };
    },
});
```

`pixi-silk/path` holds bezier paths as plain data and the helpers that edit them.
`pixi-silk/ui/pathgizmo` edits a path on screen with anchors, handles and a pen.

### Describe a path

A path is a list of anchors and a `closed` flag. Each anchor has a position, optional `in` and
`out` handles (offsets from the anchor) and a type.

```ts

const drop: BezierPath = {
    closed: true,
    anchors: [
        { x: 0, y: -80, type: 'corner' },
        { x: 50, y: 20, in: { x: 0, y: -40 }, out: { x: 0, y: 40 }, type: 'smooth' },
        { x: -50, y: 20, in: { x: 0, y: 40 }, out: { x: 0, y: -40 }, type: 'smooth' },
    ],
};
```

| Type | Handles |
|---|---|
| `corner` | Each handle moves on its own. With no handles the anchor is a sharp corner. |
| `smooth` | The two handles stay on one line, each with its own length. |
| `mirrored` | The two handles stay on one line and have the same length. |

The path fits in JSON, in a signal and in an undo step as it is.

### Draw it

```ts

declare const drop: BezierPath;

const g = new SilkGraphics();

drawPath(g, drop).fill(linear([0x64d2ff, 0x0a84ff])).stroke({ width: 2, color: 0xffffff });
```

`drawPath(g, path)` calls `moveTo`, `bezierCurveTo` and `closePath` on the graphics and returns
it, so `fill()` and `stroke()` chain on. A free path fills even-odd. See
[Lines and paths](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/lines-and-paths/).

### Edit it from code

The helpers never change their input. Each one returns a new path.

| Helper | What it does |
|---|---|
| `insertAnchor(path, segment, t)` | Splits a segment at `t` with de Casteljau's construction. The curve keeps its shape. |
| `removeAnchors`, `moveAnchors` | Delete or move a set of anchors. |
| `setHandle` | Moves one handle and keeps the twin in line on smooth and mirrored anchors. |
| `setAnchorType`, `toggleAnchorType` | Switch between corners and curves. A corner with no handles grows them from its neighbours. |
| `nearestOnPath`, `pathBounds`, `anchorsInRect` | Measure and hit-test. |
| `parseSvgPath`, `pathToSvg` | Read SVG path data, arcs and quadratics included, and write it back. |
| `mapPath` | Moves a path through an affine map. |

### Edit it on screen

```ts

declare const world: Container, overlay: Container, drop: BezierPath;

const history = new History();
const gizmo = new PathGizmo({ path: drop, space: world, history });
const shape = new SilkGraphics();

world.addChild(shape);
overlay.addChild(gizmo);
watch(gizmo.state.path, (path) => drawPath(shape.clear(), path).fill(0x0a84ff));
```

The gizmo does not draw the shape. It edits `state.path`, and the `watch` redraws the shape from
it. Put the gizmo in an overlay and set `space` to the container the path lives in, usually the
camera's world. Anchors and handles then keep their size on screen at every zoom.

These are the gestures:

- Click an anchor to select it, Shift + click to add more, and drag to move them all.
- Drag a handle to bend the curve. Alt + drag breaks the symmetry.
- Double-click an anchor to switch between a corner and a smooth point.
- Click a segment to add an anchor there.
- Delete or Backspace removes the selected anchors. Arrow keys nudge them, Shift by 10.

| Option | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `space` | the gizmo's parent | The container the path's coordinates are in. |
| `tool` | `'edit'` | `'pen'` starts with the pen. |
| `history` | none | Records every edit as one undo step. |
| `snap` | none | Moves a dragged point, for a grid or smart guides. |
| `keys` | `true` | Delete, Backspace, Escape, Enter and the arrow keys. |
| `onChange` | none | Called on every change, drags included. |
| `onCommit` | none | Called once per finished edit, with its label. |

`selectInRect(rect)` selects the anchors inside a rectangle, the hook for a marquee. `setClosed()`
closes or opens the path.

### Draw with the pen

Set `state.tool` to `'pen'`. Click for a corner, or drag for a curve with mirrored handles. Alt
during a drag moves only the outgoing handle. Click the last anchor to drop its outgoing handle,
and click the first one to close the path. Escape or Enter stops drawing.

`PenTool` is the pen's state machine with no pointer events and no drawing. Feed it presses,
moves and releases to build paths from any input, such as a recorded gesture or a test.

### Undo

With a `history`, a whole drag, an inserted point, a pen click and a run of nudges are one step
each. The gizmo needs only the `HistoryLike` interface, so your own store works too. See
[Gizmos and selection](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/gizmos-and-selection/#undo-and-redo).

---

# Colour pickers

URL: https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/color-pickers/
Area: Editor kit

> Hue rings, a colour wheel, an OKLCH plane, channel sliders, swatches, a radial swatch popup and an eyedropper for PixiJS, all editing one colour model.

> **NOTE**
> Experimental in 1.0: the API may change in a minor release.

Demo sources run in the docs demo runner, `defineDemo({ setup({ stage, params, tick }) })`. In your own app, `stage` is `app.stage` (or a container on it), `params` holds the values of the demo controls, and `tick(fn)` is `app.ticker.add`, with `t` and `dt` in seconds.

Live demo `color-rings` (source):

```ts
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { ColorModel, ColorWheel, HueSquarePicker, HueTrianglePicker, SwatchPalette } from 'pixi-silk/ui/color';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Three pickers bound to one model: drag any of them and the others follow. Releasing a drag
// commits the colour into the recent row at the bottom.
export default defineDemo({
    title: 'Colour rings',
    size: [740, 330],
    touchAction: 'none',
    setup({ silk, stage, tick }) {
        const ticker = silk.app.ticker;
        const model = new ColorModel({ color: 0x30d158 });
        const triangle = new HueTrianglePicker({ model, size: 220, ticker });
        const square = new HueSquarePicker({ model, size: 220, ticker });
        const wheel = new ColorWheel({ model, size: 196, ticker });
        const recent = new SwatchPalette({ model, columns: 12, size: 26, gap: 8, ticker });
        const chip = new SilkGraphics();

        triangle.position.set(20, 16);
        square.position.set(262, 16);
        wheel.position.set(506, 28);
        recent.position.set(64, 278);
        chip.position.set(20, 278);
        stage.addChild(triangle, square, wheel, recent, chip);
        for (const c of [0xff453a, 0xffd60a, 0x0a84ff]) {
            model.setHex(c);
            model.commit();
        }
        model.setHex(0x30d158);

        tick(() => {
            chip.clear().roundRect(0, 0, 32, 26, 8).fill(model.hex);
        });

        return undefined;
    },
});
```

Every picker in `pixi-silk/ui/color` edits a `ColorModel`. Pickers that share a model stay in sync.

### Share one colour between pickers

```ts

declare const brush: { tint: number };
declare function save(color: number): void;

const model = new ColorModel({ color: 0x30d158 });
const triangle = new HueTrianglePicker({ model, size: 220 });
const wheel = new ColorWheel({ model, size: 196 });
const recent = new SwatchPalette({ model });

model.value.subscribe(() => (brush.tint = model.hex));
model.committed.subscribe(() => save(model.hex));
```

`model.value` changes on every move of a drag, so the brush follows live. When a drag ends, the
picker calls `commit()`. That adds the colour to `model.history` and bumps `committed`, so `save`
runs once per release. A `SwatchPalette` with no `colors` shows that history.

The model stores hue, saturation, value and alpha. The hue therefore survives greys and black,
where RGB would lose it. `hex`, `rgb` and `oklch` read the colour, and `setHex()`, `setRgb()`,
`setHsv()` and `setOklch()` write it.

| Option | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `color` | `0x0a84ff` | Starting colour. |
| `alpha` | `1` | Starting alpha. |
| `historySize` | `16` | How many committed colours `history` keeps. |

### Pick a picker

| Class | What it shows |
|---|---|
| `HueTrianglePicker` | A hue ring around a triangle with the pure hue, white and black at its corners. The triangle turns to face its hue. |
| `HueSquarePicker` | A hue ring around a saturation and value square. |
| `ColorWheel` | Hue around a disc, saturation from the centre out, value on a strip beside it. |
| `OklchPicker` | Lightness against chroma for one OKLCH hue, plus a hue strip. |
| `ColorSlider`, `ChannelSliders` | Gradient tracks for HSV, RGB, OKLCH and alpha. Each track shows what its positions would give with the rest of the colour unchanged. |
| `SwatchPalette` | Fixed colours or the recent ones, as a grid or a ring. |
| `SwatchPopup` | A ring of swatches that opens around the pointer. |
| `Eyedropper` | Samples the scene through a function you supply. |

A drag belongs to the part it started on. A drag that starts on the ring only turns the hue, even
when it crosses the square.

### Pick in OKLCH

Live demo `color-oklch` (source):

```ts
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { ChannelSliders, ColorModel, checker, OklchPicker } from 'pixi-silk/ui/color';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Lightness against chroma for one OKLCH hue. The grey area is outside sRGB; dragging into it
// slides along the edge. The sliders edit the same colour in the chosen space.
export default defineDemo({
    title: 'OKLCH picker',
    size: [600, 260],
    touchAction: 'none',
    controls: {
        sliders: { type: 'select', label: 'sliders', options: ['oklch', 'hsv', 'rgb'], value: 'oklch' },
    },
    setup({ silk, stage, params, tick }) {
        const ticker = silk.app.ticker;
        const model = new ColorModel({ color: 0xbf5af2 });
        const plane = new OklchPicker({ model, width: 260, height: 180, ticker });
        const preview = new SilkGraphics();
        let mode = '';
        let sliders: ChannelSliders | null = null;

        plane.position.set(20, 20);
        preview.position.set(330, 196);
        stage.addChild(plane, preview);

        tick(() => {
            if (params.sliders !== mode) {
                mode = params.sliders;
                sliders?.destroy();
                sliders = new ChannelSliders({ model, mode: params.sliders as 'oklch', width: 220, ticker });
                sliders.position.set(330, 24);
                stage.addChild(sliders);
            }
            preview.clear();
            checker(preview, 22, 0, 220, 36, 10, 9);
            preview.roundRect(22, 0, 220, 36, 10).fill({ color: model.hex, alpha: model.alpha });
        });

        return undefined;
    },
});
```

```ts

const model = new ColorModel();
const plane = new OklchPicker({ model, width: 260, height: 180 });
const sliders = new ChannelSliders({ model, mode: 'oklch' });
```

OKLCH is lightness, chroma and hue in a space built to match how colours look. Moving lightness
does not shift the hue, and equal steps look equal. Not every OKLCH colour fits on a screen. The
picker greys out the part outside sRGB and draws its edge, and a drag into the grey part slides
along that edge. `ChannelSliders` also takes `mode: 'hsv'` or `'rgb'`, with an alpha track by
default.

### Pick from swatches or the scene

Live demo `color-popup` (source):

```ts
import { Container } from 'pixi.js';
import { horizontal, SilkGraphics, vertical } from 'pixi-silk';
import { SilkButton } from 'pixi-silk/ui/button';
import { ColorModel, Eyedropper, hsvToRgb, rgbToHex, SwatchPalette, SwatchPopup } from 'pixi-silk/ui/color';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Press anywhere on the artwork for the swatch ring: drag to a colour and let go, or tap to keep
// it open. The pipette samples the artwork (the app supplies the sampler, here plain maths).
const W = 600;
const H = 260;
const ART = { x: 20, y: 20, w: 420, h: 220 };
/** Colour of the artwork at a design-space point: hue across, value down. */
const artColor = (x: number, y: number): number | null => {
    const u = (x - ART.x) / ART.w;
    const v = (y - ART.y) / ART.h;

    return u < 0 || u > 1 || v < 0 || v > 1 ? null : rgbToHex(hsvToRgb(u * 360, 0.85, 1 - v * 0.8));
};
const pipette = (g: SilkGraphics, s: number) => {
    g.line(-s * 0.32, s * 0.32, s * 0.12, -s * 0.12).stroke({ width: s * 0.16, cap: 'round', color: 0xffffff });
    g.circle(s * 0.22, -s * 0.22, s * 0.16).fill(0xffffff);
};

export default defineDemo({
    title: 'Colour popup',
    size: [W, H],
    touchAction: 'none',
    setup({ silk, stage }) {
        const ticker = silk.app.ticker;
        const model = new ColorModel({ color: 0xff9f0a });
        const art = new SilkGraphics();
        const surface = new Container();
        const popup = new SwatchPopup({
            model,
            colors: [0xff453a, 0xff9f0a, 0xffd60a, 0x30d158, 0x64d2ff, 0x0a84ff, 0xbf5af2, 0xffffff],
            ticker,
        });
        const chip = new SilkGraphics();
        const recent = new SwatchPalette({ model, columns: 4, size: 26, gap: 8, ticker });
        const dropper = new Eyedropper({
            model,
            ticker,
            // global canvas point -> design space of the demo
            sample: (gx, gy) => {
                const p = stage.toLocal({ x: gx, y: gy });

                return artColor(p.x, p.y);
            },
        });
        const button = new SilkButton({
            shape: 'circle',
            height: 44,
            icon: pipette,
            ticker,
            theme: { fill: 0x2c2c2e, fillHover: 0x3a3a3c, fillPress: 0x1c1c1e },
            onPress: () => dropper.start(),
        });

        art.roundRect(ART.x, ART.y, ART.w, ART.h, 14).fill({
            gradient: horizontal([0xff0000, 0xffff00, 0x00ff00, 0x00ffff, 0x0000ff, 0xff00ff, 0xff0000], {
                space: 'srgb',
            }),
        });
        art.roundRect(ART.x, ART.y, ART.w, ART.h, 14).fill({ color: 0xffffff, alpha: 0.15 });
        art.roundRect(ART.x, ART.y, ART.w, ART.h, 14).fill({
            gradient: vertical(
                [
                    [0, 0x000000, 0],
                    [1, 0x000000, 0.8],
                ],
                { space: 'srgb' },
            ),
        });
        surface.eventMode = 'static';
        surface.hitArea = { contains: (x, y) => x >= ART.x && y >= ART.y && x <= ART.x + ART.w && y <= ART.y + ART.h };
        surface.on('pointerdown', (e) => {
            const p = stage.toLocal(e.global);

            popup.openAt(p.x, p.y, e);
        });
        button.position.set(482, 42);
        chip.position.set(520, 20);
        recent.position.set(462, 96);
        stage.addChild(art, surface, chip, recent, button, popup, dropper);
        model.value.subscribe(() => chip.clear().roundRect(0, 0, 60, 44, 12).fill(model.hex));
        chip.roundRect(0, 0, 60, 44, 12).fill(model.hex);

        return undefined;
    },
});
```

```ts

declare const artwork: Container, pipette: Container;
declare function colorAt(x: number, y: number): number | null;

const model = new ColorModel();
const popup = new SwatchPopup({ model, colors: [0xff453a, 0xffd60a, 0x30d158, 0x0a84ff] });
const dropper = new Eyedropper({ model, sample: (x, y) => colorAt(x, y) });

artwork.on('pointerdown', (e) => popup.openAt(e.global.x, e.global.y, e));
pipette.on('pointertap', () => dropper.start());
```

The swatch popup works like the [radial menu](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/radial-menus-and-buttons/): press, drag
towards a colour and let go, or tap to keep it open.

The eyedropper does not read pixels itself, because your app knows its scene better. The sampler
can be a texture lookup, `renderer.extract` on a render target or plain maths. A click picks,
and Escape or a right click cancels.

### How the colours are drawn

The pickers draw with pixi-silk [gradients](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/gradients/). Hue rings are conic gradients
interpolated in sRGB, so every angle is an exact HSV hue. The square and the wheel layer white and
black ramps. The OKLCH plane is drawn row by row, and chroma along a row is a straight line in
OKLab, so each row is exact.

---

# Editor tools

URL: https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/editor-tools/
Area: Editor kit

> Action buttons that orbit the selection, a brush cursor with live resize and pen pressure, a hold-to-show mode ring and a sliding segmented switch.

> **NOTE**
> Experimental in 1.0: the API may change in a minor release.

Demo sources run in the docs demo runner, `defineDemo({ setup({ stage, params, tick }) })`. In your own app, `stage` is `app.stage` (or a container on it), `params` holds the values of the demo controls, and `tick(fn)` is `app.ticker.add`, with `t` and `dt` in seconds.

Live demo `halo` (source):

```ts
import { Container } from 'pixi.js';
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { trackGesture } from 'pixi-silk/ui/gesture';
import { ContextHalo } from 'pixi-silk/ui/halo';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Icons drawn in white around (0, 0). The halo tints them.
const copy = (g: SilkGraphics, s: number) => {
    g.roundRect(-s * 0.4, -s * 0.2, s * 0.55, s * 0.6, 3).stroke({ width: s * 0.1, color: 0xffffff });
    g.roundRect(-s * 0.15, -s * 0.45, s * 0.55, s * 0.6, 3).fill(0xffffff);
};
const trash = (g: SilkGraphics, s: number) => {
    g.roundRect(-s * 0.3, -s * 0.22, s * 0.6, s * 0.66, 3).fill(0xffffff);
    g.line(-s * 0.42, -s * 0.34, s * 0.42, -s * 0.34).stroke({ width: s * 0.1, cap: 'round', color: 0xffffff });
};
const lock = (g: SilkGraphics, s: number) => {
    g.roundRect(-s * 0.32, -s * 0.05, s * 0.64, s * 0.46, 4).fill(0xffffff);
    g.arcSweep(0, -s * 0.08, s * 0.2, Math.PI, Math.PI).stroke({ width: s * 0.1, color: 0xffffff });
};
const front = (g: SilkGraphics, s: number) => {
    g.regularPoly(0, -s * 0.12, s * 0.3, 3, 0, 2).fill(0xffffff);
    g.line(-s * 0.3, s * 0.32, s * 0.3, s * 0.32).stroke({ width: s * 0.1, cap: 'round', color: 0xffffff });
};
const paint = (g: SilkGraphics, s: number) => g.circle(0, 0, s * 0.36).fill(0xffffff);

// Click a shape to select it. Drag it around: the halo follows and turns away from the edges.
export default defineDemo({
    title: 'Selection halo',
    size: [640, 380],
    touchAction: 'none',
    controls: {
        hideWhileDragging: { type: 'toggle', label: 'hide while dragging', value: false },
    },
    setup({ silk, stage, params, tick }) {
        const world = new Container();
        const halo = new ContextHalo({
            items: [
                { id: 'copy', label: 'Duplicate', icon: copy },
                { id: 'paint', label: 'Colour', icon: paint },
                { id: 'front', label: 'Bring to front', icon: front },
                { id: 'lock', label: 'Lock', icon: lock, disabled: true },
                { id: 'delete', label: 'Delete', icon: trash },
            ],
            bounds: { x: 0, y: 0, width: 640, height: 380 },
            ticker: silk.app.ticker,
            onSelect: (item) => {
                const target = halo.state.target.value as SilkGraphics | null;

                if (!target) return;
                if (item.id === 'delete') {
                    halo.detach();
                    target.destroy();
                } else if (item.id === 'front') world.addChild(target);
                else if (item.id === 'paint') redraw(target, (Math.random() * 0xffffff) | 0);
            },
        });
        const redraw = (g: SilkGraphics, colour: number) => {
            g.clear().roundRect(-60, -40, 120, 80, 18, 0.6).fill(colour);
        };
        const backdrop = new Container();

        backdrop.eventMode = 'static';
        backdrop.hitArea = { contains: () => true };
        backdrop.on('pointerdown', () => halo.detach());
        stage.addChild(backdrop, world, halo);

        [
            [170, 190, 0x0a84ff],
            [420, 110, 0x30d158],
            [520, 300, 0xff9f0a],
        ].forEach(([x, y, colour]) => {
            const g = new SilkGraphics();
            let grab = { x: 0, y: 0 };

            redraw(g, colour);
            g.position.set(x, y);
            g.cursor = 'grab';
            world.addChild(g);
            trackGesture(g, {
                onDown: (p) => {
                    halo.attach(g);
                    grab = { x: p.x - g.x, y: p.y - g.y };
                },
                onDragStart: () => halo.state.dragging.set(true),
                onDrag: (p) => g.position.set(p.x - grab.x, p.y - grab.y),
                onDragEnd: () => halo.state.dragging.set(false),
            });
        });
        halo.attach(world.children[0]);

        tick(() => {
            halo.hideWhileDragging = params.hideWhileDragging;
        });

        return undefined;
    },
});
```

Four controls for the tool layer of an editor. `ContextHalo` is in `pixi-silk/ui/halo`,
`BrushCursor` in `pixi-silk/ui/brush`, and `ModeSwitcher` and `SegmentedSwitch` in
`pixi-silk/ui/modes`.

### Show actions around the selection

```ts

const halo = new ContextHalo({
    items: [
        { id: 'copy', label: 'Duplicate', icon: drawMove },
        { id: 'delete', label: 'Delete', icon: drawTrash },
    ],
    bounds: { x: 0, y: 0, width: app.screen.width, height: app.screen.height },
    onSelect: (item) => console.log(item.id, halo.state.target.value),
});

app.stage.addChild(halo);
halo.attach(stage.children[0]);
```

Round buttons sit on an arc around the target's box and follow its bounds every frame, so drags,
tweens and camera moves need no wiring. Near an edge of `bounds` the arc turns to the side with
room. Attaching another object pops the buttons out at the old one and back in around the new
one. Input is off while they pop.

Set `state.dragging` while the reader drags the target. The halo then follows faster, or folds
away with `hideWhileDragging: true`.

| Option | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `radius` | `18` | Radius of each button. |
| `gap` | `10` | Distance from the target's box to the buttons. |
| `direction` | above | Preferred side, as an angle in radians. |
| `bounds` | none | The rectangle the buttons stay inside. |
| `transition` | `'pop'` | `'glide'` slides the buttons to a new target instead. |

An item with `disabled: true` dims and ignores taps. Its `label` shows in a pill on hover.

### Show the brush under the pointer

Live demo `brush` (source):

```ts
import { Container, RenderTexture, Sprite } from 'pixi.js';
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { BrushCursor } from 'pixi-silk/ui/brush';
import { defineDemo, SERIES } from './runtime';

// Paint on the canvas. Hold F and move (or drag with the right button) to resize: sideways is
// size, up and down is hardness. [ and ] step the size. A pen's pressure shows as the inner disc.
// Each stroke takes the next colour of the palette.
export default defineDemo({
    title: 'Brush cursor',
    size: [720, 360],
    touchAction: 'none',
    setup({ silk, stage, width, height }) {
        const surface = new Container();
        const canvas = RenderTexture.create({ width, height, resolution: silk.resolution });
        const dab = new SilkGraphics();
        const brush = new BrushCursor({ size: 36, hardness: 0.6, surface, ticker: silk.app.ticker });
        let painting = false;
        let last: { x: number; y: number } | null = null;
        let colour = 0;

        surface.eventMode = 'static';
        surface.hitArea = { contains: (x, y) => x >= 0 && y >= 0 && x <= width && y <= height };
        stage.addChild(new Sprite(canvas), surface, brush);

        const stamp = (x: number, y: number, pressure: number) => {
            const { size, hardness, opacity } = brush.state;
            const r = (size.value / 2) * (0.35 + 0.65 * pressure);

            // soft brushes are a blurred dab, hard ones a crisp disc
            dab.clear()
                .circle(x, y, r * (0.6 + 0.4 * hardness.value))
                .fill({
                    color: SERIES[colour % SERIES.length],
                    alpha: 0.35 * opacity.value,
                    blur: r * (1 - hardness.value) * 0.6,
                });
            silk.app.renderer.render({ container: dab, target: canvas, clear: false });
        };
        const paintTo = (x: number, y: number, pressure: number) => {
            const from = last ?? { x, y };
            const step = Math.max(1, brush.state.size.value * 0.12);
            const n = Math.max(1, Math.ceil(Math.hypot(x - from.x, y - from.y) / step));

            for (let i = 1; i <= n; i++)
                stamp(from.x + ((x - from.x) * i) / n, from.y + ((y - from.y) * i) / n, pressure);
            last = { x, y };
        };

        // a few strokes to start from: a hard one, a soft one and a thin one
        const strokes: [size: number, hardness: number, path: (u: number) => [number, number]][] = [
            [40, 0.85, (u) => [80 + u * 560, 120 + Math.sin(u * Math.PI * 2) * 50]],
            [64, 0.15, (u) => [100 + u * 520, 230 + Math.sin(u * Math.PI * 2 + 2) * 40]],
            [14, 1, (u) => [140 + u * 440, 290 - Math.sin(u * Math.PI) * 30]],
        ];

        for (const [size, hardness, path] of strokes) {
            brush.state.size.set(size);
            brush.state.hardness.set(hardness);
            last = null;
            for (let i = 0; i <= 60; i++) paintTo(...path(i / 60), 0.4 + 0.6 * Math.sin((i / 60) * Math.PI));
            colour++;
        }
        brush.state.size.set(36);
        brush.state.hardness.set(0.6);
        last = null;

        surface.on('pointerdown', (e) => {
            if (e.button !== 0 || brush.state.resizing.value) return;
            painting = true;
            last = null;
            const p = stage.toLocal(e.global);

            paintTo(p.x, p.y, e.pointerType === 'pen' ? e.pressure : 0.6);
        });
        surface.on('globalpointermove', (e) => {
            if (!painting || brush.state.resizing.value) return;
            const p = stage.toLocal(e.global);

            paintTo(p.x, p.y, e.pointerType === 'pen' ? e.pressure : 0.6);
        });
        const stop = () => {
            if (painting) colour++;
            painting = false;
            last = null;
        };

        surface.on('pointerup', stop);
        surface.on('pointerupoutside', stop);

        return () => canvas.destroy(true);
    },
});
```

```ts

const brush = new BrushCursor({ size: 24, hardness: 0.8, surface: stage });

app.stage.addChild(brush);
brush.state.size.subscribe((size) => console.log('brush size', size));
```

The ring is the brush size. A dashed inner ring shows hardness, and a disc grows with pen
pressure. Small brushes get a crosshair so the hot spot stays visible. The OS cursor is hidden
over `surface`. The cursor draws the brush and does not paint: read `state` and paint with your
own code.

| Option | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `size` | `32` | Brush diameter. Clamped to `minSize` (1) and `maxSize` (500). |
| `hardness` | `0.7` | 0 is soft, 1 is hard. |
| `resizeKey` | `'KeyF'` | Hold it and move to resize. The ring stays where the resize began. `null` turns it off. |
| `rightDragResize` | `true` | A right-button drag resizes too. |
| `bracketKeys` | `true` | `[` and `]` step the size. |

`state.pressure` reads pen pressure from 0 to 1. A mouse reports 0.5 while a button is down.

### Switch modes with a held key

Live demo `modes` (source):

```ts
import { Text } from 'pixi.js';
import type { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { ModeSwitcher, SegmentedSwitch } from 'pixi-silk/ui/modes';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

type Icon = (g: SilkGraphics, s: number) => void;
const brush: Icon = (g, s) => {
    g.line(-s * 0.3, s * 0.3, s * 0.3, -s * 0.3).stroke({ width: s * 0.14, cap: 'round', color: 0xffffff });
    g.circle(-s * 0.32, s * 0.32, s * 0.14).fill(0xffffff);
};
```
